`Blit Log.bat`:

```bat
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0septerra_core"
if not exist "..\reference\SR\games\Septerra Core\SR-Septerra\SR-Septerra.exe" (
  echo Build not found.
  pause
  exit /b 1
)
copy /Y "..\reference\SR\games\Septerra Core\SR-Septerra\SR-Septerra.exe" "SR-Septerra.exe" >nul
set LEGACY_BLIT_LOG=%~dp0blit_log.txt
echo ==========================================================
echo  Recording how the game draws.
echo.
echo  Play for about a minute - walk around, open a menu,
echo  get into a fight if you like. Then close the window.
echo.
echo  Nothing else to do. The log writes itself to:
echo    %LEGACY_BLIT_LOG%
echo ==========================================================
echo.
"SR-Septerra.exe"
echo.
echo Recorded. Tell Hermes it's done.
pause

```

```bat
@echo off
REM Diagnostic run for the scaled-art path. Logs what it matches and where it draws.
REM Play into the inventory, hover the disc, then close the game.
set LEGACY_PACK_DIR=%~dp0pack
set LEGACY_SCALE_DIR=%~dp0pack\scale
set LEGACY_BLIT_HOOK=1
cd /d "%~dp0septerra_core"
SR-Septerra.exe 2> "%~dp0scale_debug.log"
echo.
echo Log written to scale_debug.log
pause

```

`Dump Game Data.bat`:

```bat
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0septerra_core"
if not exist "..\reference\SR\games\Septerra Core\SR-Septerra\SR-Septerra.exe" (
  echo Build not found. Build it first.
  pause
  exit /b 1
)
copy /Y "..\reference\SR\games\Septerra Core\SR-Septerra\SR-Septerra.exe" "SR-Septerra.exe" >nul
set LEGACY_PACK_DUMP=%~dp0dumped_data
set LEGACY_PACK_TRACE=1
if not exist "%LEGACY_PACK_DUMP%" mkdir "%LEGACY_PACK_DUMP%"
echo ==========================================================
echo  Extracting game records to:
echo    %LEGACY_PACK_DUMP%
echo.
echo  Let the game get to the main menu (or a bit further),
echo  then close the window. A log is written next to this file.
echo ==========================================================
echo.
"SR-Septerra.exe" 2> "%~dp0dump_log.txt"
echo.
echo Finished. %ERRORLEVEL%
dir /b "%LEGACY_PACK_DUMP%" | find /c /v "" > "%~dp0dump_count.txt"
echo Files extracted:
type "%~dp0dump_count.txt"
pause

```

`NOTES.md`:

```md
# Septerra Core -> native Windows port (SRW static recompilation)

Working tree for recompiling *Septerra Core* into a native 32-bit Windows executable
using [M-HT/SR](https://github.com/M-HT/SR) — specifically its Windows recompiler **SRW**.

**Current state: `SR-Septerra.exe` builds and launches.** Video is stubbed (see
*Known gaps*), so movies do nothing.

## Layout

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `reference/SR` | Git submodule, M-HT/SR @ `ac690dd`. `SRW/` = recompiler, `SRW-games/Septerra Core/SRW/` = recompile config (**upstream-tracked**), `games/Septerra Core/SR-Septerra/` = C glue + build dir |
| `septerra_core/` | Game install (GOG **1.04**) — exe, `*.db` assets, `QuickTime.qts`, plus the built `SR-Septerra.exe` and its runtime DLLs |
| `libs/` | Deps: SDL2 2.30.9, mpg123 1.24.0-x86, libquicktime 1.2.4 src (headers only), `lqt_stubs`, **nasm 2.16.03** |
| `work/` | Scratch: SRW run dirs, probe objects, `a.a`/`b.a`. Gitignored |
| `port/build/` | Reproducible build scripts (below) |
| `port/patches/` | Patch applied to the submodule |
| `ghidra_12.2_PUBLIC/`, `H:\jdk-21` | Ghidra 12.2 + JDK 21 (launcher `H:\run_ghidra.bat`); not used by the build |

## Build — three scripts, in order

```
H:\ghidra\port\build\build_lqt_stub.bat      -> port\build\out\lqt\quicktime.lib
H:\ghidra\port\build\build_mpg123_lib.bat    -> port\build\out\lib\mpg123-0.lib
H:\ghidra\port\build\build.bat               -> SR-Septerra.exe
```

`build.bat` does the whole chain: SRW recompile -> copy generated source into `x86\` ->
`compact_source.py` -> nasm probe pass -> `repair_short_jumps.py` -> `scons` (assemble +
link with MSVC). Give each script something with a console: they call `vcvarsall.bat`.

Prerequisites: MSVC BuildTools 2022 (x86), NASM 2.16.03 at `libs\nasm`, Python
(`%USERPROFILE%\miniconda3\python.exe`), SDL2 2.30.9 + mpg123 dev files under `libs\`.

### The five things that break it

1. **The `.sci` config files are mandatory.** `build-x86.sh` copies
   `SRW-games/Septerra Core/SRW/x86/*.sci` next to `SRW.exe` before running it. Skip that
   and SRW still exits 0 but emits ~3,700 `short jump is out of range` errors and no
   extern declarations (`symbol 'GetTickCount' not defined`, hundreds of them), and the
   generated source is twice the size. Copy the `.sci` files, every time.
2. **`scons` alone fails with `Cannot open include file: 'SDL.h'`.** The include/lib paths
   only reach SCons through the `PATH_INC` / `PATH_LIB` env vars (`SConstruct:125-128`).
3. **`SConstruct` needs `advapi32` and `user32` added to the `cl` LIBS list** (see
   `port/patches/0001-sconstruct-add-advapi32-user32.patch`). Upstream builds with mingw,
   which links those by default; MSVC does not, so `Reg*`/`MessageBoxA` come up unresolved.
   Apply with `git -C reference/SR apply ..\..\port\patches\0001-*.patch`.
4. **mpg123's import library must be regenerated.** Upstream's `libmpg123-0.dll.def` has no
   `LIBRARY` line, so `lib /def:libmpg123-0.dll.def` names the module after the *file* and
   appends `.dll` again — the exe ends up importing `libmpg123-0.dll.dll`. Link succeeds,
   the exe then dies instantly with `0xC0000135` (STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND). Prepend
   `LIBRARY libmpg123-0.dll` and rebuild; also keep that `.lib` in its **own** directory,
   because with the mpg123 release dir on `LIBPATH` SCons matches the DLL file instead.
5. **`quicktime.lib` is a stub**, not libquicktime.

NASM 2.15.03–2.15.05 are known-broken with SRW; 2.16.03 works.

## The game executable must be v1.04

Upstream supports only the **English 1.04** build:

```
Septerra104.exe   729088 bytes
md5     94daaeb19c17d9af3e2ae245219037a9
sha1    2dd27a46f41a91fed0e6ee7205e4b9cef61275c9
sha256  f2f6040416589935ccbc206fee4db1ef369f6c9e8d167e631b190919b12fdf91
```

**The GOG build of 1.04 works even though its hashes differ** (md5 `3e518925…`,
sha256 `dac9c8a2…`): same 729088-byte size, `FileVersion 1.04`, `goggame.dll` reports
`1.0.4.0`, different PE timestamp only. Verified by running the recompiler: **all 21,588
relocations in the upstream `relocations.csv` match the GOG binary byte-for-byte, zero
mismatches.** The hash gap is header packaging, not code.

The earlier retail CD was **1.02**: 610362 bytes, PE32, **no `.reloc` section** — hence
`Relocations were stripped from file - need external relocations`, and the relocation table
(derived from 1.04) cannot line up with it. SRW then aborts with
`Error: inconsistent relocation target address` at `SRW_loader.c:529`.

## Do not do this again

`fix_relocations.py` rewrote the target column by reading the dword at each
fixup address in the 1.02 exe. That does not recover a relocation table — 30.9% of "targets"
land inside the image versus 86.7% in the upstream table, with gems like
`0x401017 -> 0xc0850049` (a `mov` encoding, not an address). Reproduced for the record as
`games/Septerra Core/SR-Septerra/x86/relocations.csv.1.02-derived.bak`; the live
`relocations.csv` there is the pristine upstream copy (sha256 `4f20420a…`).

`patch_srw.py` / `patch_srw_unconditional.py` deleted or neutered that
`inconsistent relocation target address` check in `SRW_loader.c`. That suppresses the symptom
of a version mismatch and ships a binary with silently wrong pointers. `SRW_loader.c` is
pristine — keep it that way.

## Known gaps

- **Video playback is dead.** `port/build/lqt_stub.c` exports the 24 `lqt_*` / `quicktime_*`
  symbols QTML.c needs, all returning 0. The exe links and the game runs, but any QuickTime
  movie plays nothing. Real libquicktime needs MSYS2 + autotools + ffmpeg, or a prebuilt
  Windows `quicktime.lib` from someone who has one.
- **GUI not yet observed.** The smoke test (`port/build/smoke_test.ps1`) launches the exe over
  SSH, i.e. in session 0 with no desktop, and only proves it does not crash immediately
  (stays up >8 s). Whether it renders needs a run in the interactive RDP/console session.
- 32-bit x86 only; the `x64` and `llasm` build dirs are untouched.

## Recompiled artifact

`SR-Septerra.exe`: 439296 bytes, PE32 i386, subsystem WINDOWS, image base `0x400000`,
imports SDL2 / libmpg123-0 / quicktime(stub) / WINMM / ADVAPI32 / USER32 / KERNEL32 /
SHELL32. Runtime needs `SDL2.dll`, `libmpg123-0.dll`, `quicktime.dll` beside it.

## Decompilation (the actual source-port path)

The recomp is not the source port; it is the map plus the oracle. Ghidra work lives in
`port/ghidra/`, project in `work/ghidra-proj/Septerra`, output in `work/ghidra-out/`.

```
port\ghidra\analyze_septerra.bat   -> import + analyze + Inventory.java
port\ghidra\ground_truth.bat       -> GroundTruth.java (seed .sci truth) + labeled inventory
```

Ghidra 12.2 needs **JDK 25** (`H:\jdk-25\jdk-25.0.5+8`); the JDK 21 that
`install_ghidra.py` pulled is too old and fails with "unsupported java version". The project
directory must exist before `analyzeHeadless` runs ("Directory not found" otherwise).

### Inventory of the original 1.04 (measured)

| | count | bytes |
|---|---|---|
| functions recovered | 1335 | 429588 |
| looks like MSVC CRT (skip — link the real CRT instead) | 427 | 87061 (20%) |
| game/engine | 908 | 342527 (80%) |
| of which still unnamed `FUN_*` | 806 | 327180 |
| asm<->C boundary (`*_asm2c`, already C in the shim) | 83 | — |

Median function 114 bytes; 451 functions <=64 bytes; 88 >=1025 bytes; largest 6565 bytes
(`FUN_0043fc30`). Ground truth seeded: 84 renamed, 2 no-return, 5 missed code ranges
disassembled, 31 data ranges cleared.

**Correction to the obvious plan:** the `.db` archive reader is *not* a decompilation target.
`OpenGameDataFiles` / `RecordOpen` / `RecordRead` / `CloseGameDataFiles` are already written in
C in `Game-DataFiles.c` — the generated asm just calls them (`*_asm2c` = asm calls C). The 83
boundary functions are done; the remaining work is the 806 unnamed game/engine functions.

Next sensible step: walk the call graph down from `WinMain_` (0x412b80) to pick subsystems by
boundary rather than by address.

### Call-graph walk (step 2)

```
port\ghidra\make_symbol_map.py         -> out/shim_symbols.csv (241 boundary symbols -> subsystem)
port\ghidra\run_calltree.bat           -> calltree_nodes.csv / calltree_edges.csv
port\ghidra\run_annotate.bat           -> annotation.csv (string/xref evidence for top functions)
```

Result and the caveats that matter: **`port/ghidra/subsystem_map.md`**. Short version: 743 of
1,335 functions are reachable from `WinMain_` (264 KB), 578 of those are pure engine logic
(163 KB), and the walk is blind to indirect calls — so "unreachable" means "unknown route",
not "unused". First decompilation target: `0x430500` (5.8 KB, 38 callees, directly under
`WinMain_`).

### Typing pass on the state machine

`port/ghidra/run_typestate.bat` (TypeState.java) renames `DAT_004ae978` -> `g_gameState`
(enum `GameState`, members STATE_0..STATE_5/11/13/14) and `DAT_004ae960` -> `g_shutdown` (int,
name provisional — see the finding doc for the contradictory evidence). WinMain_ now reads as
a dispatcher. It does **not** shrink `FUN_00430500`: its switch operand is a pointer-typed
local, so cases still render as `(int *)0x0..0x4`.

```

`README.md`:

```md
# The Legacy Recreated

An unofficial decompilation workbench for **Septerra Core 1.04** (1999, GOG build) — working
notes and Ghidra tooling for turning the shipped executable into a readable, eventually
portable code base. The runnable target is the static-recompilation build
from [M-HT/SR](https://github.com/M-HT/SR) (`SR-Septerra.exe`); the decompilation work happens
on the **original** `septerra.exe`, which is the only thing worth decompiling.

This repo contains **no game data and no decompiled game code**. You need your own copy of the
game; nothing here redistributes it.

## Why this exists

I played Septerra Core as a demo, in the window after Valkyrie had lost their publisher and
before they'd found another. The developers asked for feedback, so I wrote back saying a
console-style JRPG on PC was exactly what I wanted and I'd buy it on release. They asked to
quote my email in their pitch. I agreed — which means that in a very small way, I helped put
this game in a box.

That's the honest reason this repo exists. Not preservation in the abstract: I want to play the
game I helped get made, at a resolution that isn't 1999.

The timeline behind that — Viacom New Media closing, Valkyrie reforming, Monolith signing — is
in [`port/docs/history.md`](port/docs/history.md), with sources.

## Approach

The recompilation is not a source port — it is a translation of a translation. Two facts make
it useful anyway:

1. `SRW` proves you have the right binary: all 21,588 relocations resolve byte-for-byte.
2. The ~16,000 lines of C shim in `reference/SR` (SDL2, Win32, DirectDraw, DirectSound) is the
   portability layer. Decompiled code that links against it runs on modern hardware.

So: keep the recomp as a **behavioural oracle**, decompile the original for **readable C**, and
replace generated assembly function-by-function. The build stays runnable at every step.

## Requirements

| | |
|---|---|
| Game | Septerra Core 1.04, `septerra.exe` = 729,088 bytes, PE32 i386, image base `0x400000` |
| Hash | md5 `94daaeb19c17d9af3e2ae245219037a9` — the GOG repack differs but content matches (proven by relocation match) |
| Ghidra | 12.2 (needs **JDK 25**; it refuses 21) |
| Build | NASM 2.16, MSVC BuildTools 2022 x86, Python for `scons` |
| Submodule | `reference/SR` (M-HT/SR, pinned) — provides SRW and the shim |

## Pipeline

```
septerra.exe ──SRW──> Septerra.asm + seg*.inc ──nasm──> .obj ──MSVC──> SR-Septerra.exe  (oracle)
      │
      └──Ghidra──> project ──Inventory──> function inventory (1,335 functions / 429,588 bytes)
                       │
                       ├── GroundTruth.java   seed names from SRW's .sci files
                       ├── CallTree.java      reachability + subsystem tagging from WinMain_
                       ├── Decompile.java     decompile one or many functions + metrics
                       ├── TypeState.java     apply every established name, type and struct
                       ├── Structs.java       create ObjSlot/Effect/Obj + emit objslot.h
                       └── Xrefs.java         who reads/writes this global
```

`port/build/build.bat` is the single entry point for the recomp; each Ghidra script has a
matching `run_*.bat`.

## Findings

Each of these is backed by disassembly or by code in the binary, not by pattern-guessing:

- **Two-level state machine.** `WinMain_` dispatches on `g_gameState` (states 3/4/5 →
  `FUN_00430500`); that function dispatches on `g_subState` (phases `SUB_0..SUB_12`).
  `SUB_8` is the level-entry spawn pass, `SUB_9` the per-frame update.
- **Object slots, 0xBE8 bytes.** `BuildObjTables` proves it: `memset(slot,0,0xbe8)`,
  `slot[0] = object`, selector at `slot+0xB10`, stride `0x2fa` ints. Five 0x1C8 effect
  sub-structs at `slot+0x228`.
- **The object header is the `Effect` layout** — every object offset touched is below `0x1C8`.
- **Combat lists, named by the game's own assert strings.** `AddCombatant` errors with
  *"Tell Jim you ran out of slots in the Allies Combat List"* / *"…the Enemies Combat List"*,
  both capped at 10. Allies occupy slots 3–12 (0–2 reserved), enemies get a second slot array.
- **`g_flag_ae960` is a dud.** 19 references, all reads, zero writes, initial value 0 — it was
  named `g_shutdown` in an earlier pass and the name was wrong. Negative results are recorded
  because they cost the most time to rediscover.

`port/ghidra/finding_430500_state_machine.md` has the full write-up, including two things that
**did not** work: retyping a switch's *global* never clears `case (int *)0x0:` labels, and
`case` labels survive retyping the operand's variable because Ghidra emits the switch expression
inline.

## Asset packs

`port/docs/pack_format.md` specifies the replacement-art format (draft v0.1): where art enters
the engine (`RecordTryOpen` → `RecordRead`), the 0x230-byte animation frame record, and the
staged capability plan — whole-blob overrides work on the current build, per-frame overrides
need a hook, and larger-than-original art needs a scaling blit that the engine does not have.
The format ships; the art never does.

## Layout

```
NOTES.md                  environment, build chain, every trap hit so far
port/build/               recomp pipeline: stub libs, mpg123 import lib, build.bat
port/ghidra/              Ghidra scripts + .bat wrappers + findings
port/include/             engine headers derived from the binary (objslot.h)
port/docs/                format specs (pack_format.md)
port/patches/             the single SConstruct patch needed by the shim
reference/SR              upstream M-HT/SR (submodule)
work/                     scratch: SRW runs, Ghidra project, decompiled output (gitignored)
```

## Status

Readable so far: the state machine, object/effect/slot layout, spawn and combat registration.
Not started: graphics, audio, input, script interpreter, menus. No function has been ported to C
yet — the immediate goal is coverage of the per-frame path, not source yet.

## Trademark & legal

Unofficial fan research project. Not affiliated with, endorsed by, or sponsored by the rights
holders of Septerra Core. "Septerra Core" is used only to identify the software being studied.

This repository ships **no game data, no game executable, and no decompiled game code**. You
must own a copy of the game to use any of it. Nothing here enables playing the game without
one.

## Licence

MIT for the tooling and notes (see `LICENSE`). It covers neither the game, its data, nor any
decompiled output — those are not distributed here.

```

`Run Game.bat`:

```bat
@echo off
setlocal
cd /d "%~dp0septerra_core"
if not exist "SR-Septerra.exe" (
  echo Copying the modern build...
  copy /Y "..\reference\SR\games\Septerra Core\SR-Septerra\SR-Septerra.exe" "SR-Septerra.exe" >nul
)
echo ==========================================================
echo  Septerra Core - modern build
echo  window size is set in: %CD%\Septerra.cfg
echo  change Display_Width / Display_Height there to resize
echo ==========================================================
echo.
"SR-Septerra.exe"
echo.
echo Game exited with code %ERRORLEVEL%.
pause

```

`Test Pack.bat`:


`port/build/build.bat`:

```bat
@echo off
rem ===========================================================================
rem Septerra Core -> SR-Septerra.exe, full pipeline (Windows x86, MSVC + NASM).
rem
rem   step 1  port\build\build_lqt_stub.bat    -> out\lqt\quicktime.lib (STUB)
rem   step 2  port\build\build_mpg123_lib.bat  -> out\lib\mpg123-0.lib
rem   step 3  this script                      -> SR-Septerra.exe
rem
rem Requires (see NOTES.md): Ghidra tree at H:\ghidra, JDK not needed here,
rem NASM 2.16.03 under libs\nasm, MSVC BuildTools 2022, Python for the helper
rem scripts, and septerra_core\septerra.exe = English 1.04 (GOG build accepted).
rem ===========================================================================
setlocal
set "HERE=%~dp0"
set "ROOT=H:\ghidra"
set "SR=%ROOT%\reference\SR"
set "GAME=%SR%\games\Septerra Core\SR-Septerra"
set "XD=%GAME%\x86"
set "RUN=%ROOT%\work\srw-run2"
set "NASMDIR=%ROOT%\libs\nasm\nasm-2.16.03"
set "NASM=%NASMDIR%\nasm.exe"
rem python: override with  set "PY=C:\path\to\python.exe"
if not defined PY set "PY=%USERPROFILE%\miniconda3\python.exe"
if not exist "%PY%" set "PY=python"
set "PATH=%NASMDIR%;%PATH%"

rem ---- 1. static recompile: 1.04 exe + config -> Septerra.asm + seg*.inc ------
if not exist "%RUN%" mkdir "%RUN%"
copy /y "%SR%\SRW\SRW.exe"                             "%RUN%\" >nul
copy /y "%ROOT%\septerra_core\septerra.exe"            "%RUN%\Septerra104.exe" >nul
copy /y "%SR%\SRW-games\Septerra Core\SRW\SR.cfg"        "%RUN%\" >nul
copy /y "%SR%\SRW-games\Septerra Core\SRW\bssborder.csv" "%RUN%\" >nul
copy /y "%SR%\SRW-games\Septerra Core\SRW\relocations.csv" "%RUN%\" >nul
rem The .sci files are MANDATORY: without them SRW emits ~3,700 short-jump
rem errors and drops all the extern declarations (undefined GetTickCount etc).
copy /y "%SR%\SRW-games\Septerra Core\SRW\x86\*.sci"     "%RUN%\" >nul

cd /d "%RUN%"
"%RUN%\SRW.exe" Septerra104.exe Septerra.asm
if errorlevel 1 goto :srwfail

rem ---- 2. generated source into the build dir ------------------------------
rem one copy per file: cmd's copy concatenates multiple sources into one target
copy /y "%RUN%\Septerra.asm"        "%XD%\" >nul
copy /y "%RUN%\seg01.inc"           "%XD%\" >nul
copy /y "%RUN%\seg02.inc"           "%XD%\" >nul
copy /y "%RUN%\seg03.inc"           "%XD%\" >nul
copy /y "%RUN%\seg05.inc"           "%XD%\" >nul
copy /y "%RUN%\Septerra.resdump"    "%XD%\" >nul
cd /d "%XD%"

"%PY%" compact_source.py
if errorlevel 1 goto :fail

rem ---- 3. nasm probe pass, repair out-of-range short jumps, real build -------
rem nasm's stderr is what repair_short_jumps.py parses (build-x86.sh does the
rem same with a.a); the probe object is thrown away, scons assembles for real.
"%NASM%" -fwin32 -O1 -w+orphan-labels -w-number-overflow -ix86/ Septerra.asm -o "%TEMP%\septerra_probe.obj" 2>a.a
"%PY%" repair_short_jumps.py
if errorlevel 1 goto :fail

call "C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\VC\Auxiliary\Build\vcvarsall.bat" x86
set "PATH_INC=%ROOT%\libs\SDL2\SDL2-2.30.9\include;%ROOT%\libs\mpg123\mpg123-1.24.0-x86;%ROOT%\libs\libquicktime-src\include;%ROOT%\libs\lqt_stubs"
set "PATH_LIB=%HERE%out\lqt;%HERE%out\lib;%ROOT%\libs\SDL2\SDL2-2.30.9\lib\x86"

cd /d "%GAME%"
del /q SR-Septerra.exe 2>nul
scons
if errorlevel 1 goto :fail
echo === built: %GAME%\SR-Septerra.exe ===
exit /b 0

:srwfail
echo SRW_RECOMPILE_FAILED
exit /b 1
:fail
echo BUILD_FAILED
exit /b 1

```

`port/build/build_lqt_stub.bat`:

```bat
@echo off
rem ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
rem Step 1 of 3: build the link-time libquicktime stub (quicktime.dll + .lib).
rem SR-Septerra's QTML.c calls libquicktime (lqt_*/quicktime_*). Real libquicktime
rem for Windows needs MSYS2 + autotools + ffmpeg, so this stub exists only to let
rem the exe LINK. Every entry point returns 0 at runtime: movie playback does
rem nothing. Test builds only.
rem ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
set "HERE=%~dp0"
set "OUT=%HERE%out\lqt"
call "C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\VC\Auxiliary\Build\vcvarsall.bat" x86
if not exist "%OUT%" mkdir "%OUT%"
cd /d "%HERE%"

del /q "%OUT%\quicktime.dll" "%OUT%\quicktime.lib" "%OUT%\quicktime.exp" "%OUT%\lqt_stub.obj" 2>nul
cl /nologo /O2 /c lqt_stub.c /Fo:"%OUT%\lqt_stub.obj"
if errorlevel 1 goto :fail
link /nologo /DLL /MACHINE:X86 /OUT:"%OUT%\quicktime.dll" /DEF:quicktime.def "%OUT%\lqt_stub.obj"
if errorlevel 1 goto :fail
echo === stub ok: %OUT%\quicktime.dll ===
dir /b "%OUT%\quicktime.dll" "%OUT%\quicktime.lib"
goto :eof
:fail
echo STUB_BUILD_FAILED
exit /b 1

```

`port/build/build_mpg123_lib.bat`:

```bat
@echo off
rem ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
rem Step 2 of 3: regenerate mpg123's import library with an explicit DLL name.
rem Upstream's libmpg123-0.dll.def has NO "LIBRARY" line, so 'lib /def:' names the
rem module after the FILE (libmpg123-0.dll) and adds .dll again, producing an
rem import for "libmpg123-0.dll.dll". The exe then links fine but dies at load
rem with 0xC0000135 (DLL not found). Fix: prepend the LIBRARY line.
rem ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
set "HERE=%~dp0"
set "OUT=%HERE%out\lib"
set "SRC=H:\ghidra\libs\mpg123\mpg123-1.24.0-x86\libmpg123-0.dll.def"
call "C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\2022\BuildTools\VC\Auxiliary\Build\vcvarsall.bat" x86
if not exist "%OUT%" mkdir "%OUT%"
cd /d "%OUT%"

> mpg123-0.def echo LIBRARY libmpg123-0.dll
>>mpg123-0.def type "%SRC%"

del /q mpg123-0.lib 2>nul
lib /nologo /def:mpg123-0.def /out:mpg123-0.lib /machine:x86
if errorlevel 1 goto :fail
echo === mpg123 import lib ok: %OUT%\mpg123-0.lib ===
goto :eof
:fail
echo MPG123_LIB_FAILED
exit /b 1

```

`port/build/lqt_stub.c`:

```c
/*
 * lqt_stub — link-time stub for libquicktime's Windows import library.
 *
 * SR-Septerra's QTML.c decodes the game's QuickTime movies through libquicktime
 * (lqt_open_read, lqt_decode_video, ...). Building real libquicktime for Windows
 * needs MSYS2 + autotools + ffmpeg, so this stub exists only to let the recompiled
 * exe LINK so the rest of the port can be exercised. At runtime every entry point
 * returns 0/NULL: anything that plays a movie will do nothing (and may fail), while
 * the rest of the game is unaffected.
 *
 * All symbols are cdecl in libquicktime, so a definition that ignores its arguments
 * is safe — the caller pops the stack.
 *
 * NOT a substitute for the real library. Ship SR-Septerra.exe linked against this
 * only as a test build.
 */

long lqt_open_read(void)              { return 0; }
long lqt_decode_video(void)           { return 0; }
long lqt_decode_audio_raw(void)       { return 0; }
long lqt_decode_audio_track(void)     { return 0; }
long lqt_frame_time(void)             { return 0; }
long lqt_last_audio_position(void)    { return 0; }
long lqt_get_best_colormodel(void)    { return 0; }
long lqt_get_sample_format(void)      { return 0; }
long lqt_rows_alloc(void)             { return 0; }
long lqt_rows_free(void)              { return 0; }
long lqt_set_cmodel(void)             { return 0; }
long lqt_video_duration(void)         { return 0; }
long lqt_video_time_scale(void)       { return 0; }
long lqt_close(void)                  { return 0; }

/* legacy libquicktime API (Qt/handlers) */
long quicktime_close(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_audio_length(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_audio_tracks(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_video_tracks(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_sample_rate(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_track_channels(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_video_width(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_video_height(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_supported_video(void) { return 0; }
long quicktime_supported_audio(void) { return 0; }

```
