`port/patches/README.md`:

```md
# Shim patches

`reference/SR` is a pinned third-party submodule. Every edit we make to it lives here as a patch,
so the submodule can be reset at any time and our overlay rebuilt from files that are actually
committed to this repo.

## Which patch to apply

**`0008-current-overlay.patch` is the one that matters.** It is the complete diff of the working
tree against the pinned submodule commit, verified with `git apply --check --reverse` (the check
passing means the patch reproduces our tree exactly). Apply it to a pristine submodule and you get
the exact build environment used in `port/docs/renderer.md`.

```
cd reference/SR
git apply ../../port/patches/0008-current-overlay.patch
```

## Historical increments

`0001`-`0007` are kept for the record: each one added a single capability at the time it was
built. They are *not* a clean sequence to reapply — they were written against earlier trees.
Read them for history, apply `0008` to build.

| Patch | What it added |
|---|---|
| 0001 | `advapi32` + `user32` link libraries in SConstruct |
| 0002 | blob-level pack override in `Game-DataFiles.c` (`LEGACY_PACK_DIR`) |
| 0003 | blit logging (`LEGACY_BLIT_LOG`) |
| 0004 | frame capture + scripted input (`LEGACY_SHOT_*`, `LEGACY_INPUT`) |
| 0005 | mouse click injection (`Patch_PushMouseClick`) |
| 0006 | surface dump (`LEGACY_DUMP_SURFACES`) |
| 0007 | whole-surface art override (`LEGACY_ART`) — proved the shortcut does not exist |

## What 0008 adds on top

- `/MAP` link flag — produces `SR-Septerra.map`, 12,399 named symbols, the address/name bridge
  between runtime values and original functions.
- `LEGACY_CALLERS` / `LEGACY_BT` — direct-caller and stack reporting at the DirectDraw entry points.
- `LEGACY_FIND_GLOBAL` — scans engine data for whoever stored the screen surface pointer.
- `LEGACY_IMAGE_DIR` / `LEGACY_FIND_IMAGE` — live image-struct dump and key search, used to solve
  the AM04 palette format.
- `LEGACY_CACHE_DUMP`, `LEGACY_SLOTS` — image cache and slot draining.

```

`port/tools/am04_info.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""am04_info.py - read Septerra Core AM04 image blobs without the game.

Format, per the loader (FUN_0040f000 in the original 1.04 binary), which
relocates eight offsets to pointers at load time:

  +0x00  char[4]  'AM04'
  +0x04  uint32   type/flags
  +0x08  uint32   offset -> section A
  +0x10  uint32   offset -> section B
  +0x18  uint32   offset -> frame record array   (0x400 bytes per frame,
                                   i.e. 256 colour entries of 4 bytes)
  +0x1C  uint32   number of frames
  +0x20  uint32   offset -> section D
  +0x28  uint32   offset -> section E (row descriptors)
  +0x30  uint32   offset -> section F
  +0x38  uint32   offset -> section G
  +0x40  uint32   offset -> section H (pixel pool)

Each frame record:

  +0x000  uint32[256]  palette, 0x00BBGGRR per entry  (1024 bytes = 0x400)
  +0x400  next record

Verified against the live engine: the loaded copy matches the file byte for byte
except eight relocated offsets, the frame array pointer (replaced at runtime with
the engine's own converted copy) and the type dword (forced to 0xFFFFFFFF). Record
span / frame count is exactly 0x400 on every file checked.

Usage:
    python3 am04_info.py <file.bin> [--palette out.ppm]
"""

import struct
import sys

RECORD = 0x400   # 256 entries x 4 bytes (0x00BBGGRR)
PALETTE_OFF = 0x00


def rgb(c):
    """0x00BBGGRR -> (r, g, b)"""
    return (c & 0xFF, (c >> 8) & 0xFF, (c >> 16) & 0xFF)


def read_am04(data):
    if data[:4] != b"AM04":
        raise ValueError("not an AM04 blob (magic %r)" % data[:4])
    head = {
        "type": struct.unpack_from("<I", data, 0x04)[0],
        "frames": struct.unpack_from("<I", data, 0x1C)[0],
        "sections": {},
    }
    for name, off in (("A", 0x08), ("B", 0x10), ("records", 0x18), ("D", 0x20),
                      ("E", 0x28), ("F", 0x30), ("G", 0x38), ("H", 0x40)):
        head["sections"][name] = struct.unpack_from("<I", data, off)[0]
    rec_base = head["sections"]["records"]
    n = head["frames"]
    if n == 0 or rec_base + n * RECORD > len(data):
        raise ValueError("frame array runs past end of file (%d frames at 0x%x, file 0x%x)"
                         % (n, rec_base, len(data)))
    head["palettes"] = []
    for i in range(n):
        r = rec_base + i * RECORD
        head["palettes"].append(struct.unpack_from("<256I", data, r))
    return head


def write_palette_ppm(head, path):
    rows = []
    for pal in head["palettes"]:
        line = b"".join(bytes(rgb(c)) * 16 for c in pal)
        rows.extend([line] * 16)
    w, h = 256 * 16, len(rows)
    with open(path, "wb") as f:
        f.write(b"P6\n%d %d\n255\n" % (w, h))
        for line in rows:
            f.write(line)
    return path


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    path = sys.argv[1]
    data = open(path, "rb").read()
    head = read_am04(data)
    print("file      : %s (%d bytes)" % (path, len(data)))
    print("type      : 0x%x" % head["type"])
    print("frames    : %d" % head["frames"])
    print("sections  :")
    for name, off in head["sections"].items():
        note = "  (empty)" if off == 0 else ("  (OUTSIDE FILE)" if off >= len(data) else "")
        print("   %-8s 0x%06x%s" % (name, off, note))
    distinct = len(set(head["palettes"][0]))
    print("palette[0] : first 8 = %s, %d distinct" %
          (" ".join("%06x" % (c & 0xFFFFFF) for c in head["palettes"][0][:8]), distinct))
    if "--palette" in sys.argv:
        out = sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--palette") + 1]
        print("wrote     : %s" % write_palette_ppm(head, out))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`port/tools/am04_replace.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""am04_replace.py - replace one sprite frame inside an AM04 file, in place.

Keeps the file structurally identical: same sections, same offsets, same run
descriptors, same size. Only the index bytes belonging to the chosen entry are
rewritten, so the engine loads it exactly as it loaded the original.

Usage:
    python3 am04_replace.py FILE --entry 9 --pattern stripes --out NEW.bin
    python3 am04_replace.py FILE --entry 9 --palette 16,4,17 --out NEW.bin
"""
import argparse
import struct


def sections(data):
    get = lambda o: struct.unpack_from('<I', data, o)[0]
    return {'pal': get(0x18), 'D': get(0x20), 'E': get(0x28),
            'F': get(0x30), 'G': get(0x38)}


def entry_geometry(data, sec, entry):
    o = sec['D'] + entry * 0x18
    packed = struct.unpack_from('<I', data, o)[0]
    h, w = packed >> 16, packed & 0xFFFF
    row_start = struct.unpack_from('<i', data, o + 0x10)[0]
    entry_w = struct.unpack_from('<i', data, o + 0x14)[0]
    return (entry_w or w), h, row_start


def pixels(data, sec, entry):
    """Yield (file_offset, x, y) for every index byte this entry draws."""
    w, h, row_start = entry_geometry(data, sec, entry)
    out = []
    for y in range(h):
        ro = sec['G'] + (row_start + y) * 8
        if ro + 8 > len(data):
            break
        first, runs = struct.unpack_from('<II', data, ro)
        if runs > 4096:
            continue
        x = 0
        for r in range(runs):
            do = sec['F'] + (first + r) * 8
            if do + 8 > len(data):
                break
            off, skip, count = struct.unpack_from('<IHH', data, do)
            x += skip
            for i in range(count):
                p = sec['E'] + off + i
                if p >= len(data):
                    break
                out.append((p, x, y))
                x += 1
    return out, w, h


def vivid_indices(data, sec, count):
    """Pick the most saturated palette entries, leaving index 0 alone."""
    pal = [struct.unpack_from('<I', data, sec['pal'] + i * 4)[0] for i in range(256)]
    def sat(c):
        r, g, b = c & 255, (c >> 8) & 255, (c >> 16) & 255
        return max(r, g, b) - min(r, g, b)
    ranked = sorted(range(1, 256), key=lambda i: -sat(pal[i]))
    return ranked[:count]


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument('file')
    ap.add_argument('--entry', type=int, required=True)
    ap.add_argument('--pattern', default='stripes')
    ap.add_argument('--palette', default='')
    ap.add_argument('--out', required=True)
    a = ap.parse_args()

    data = bytearray(open(a.file, 'rb').read())
    if data[:4] != b'AM04':
        raise SystemExit('not an AM04 file')

    sec = sections(data)
    pts, w, h = pixels(data, sec, a.entry)
    if not pts:
        raise SystemExit('entry %d draws nothing' % a.entry)

    if a.palette:
        cols = [int(v) for v in a.palette.split(',')]
    else:
        cols = vivid_indices(data, sec, 4)

    from collections import Counter
    dominant = Counter(data[off] for (off, x, y) in pts).most_common(1)[0][0]
    mask = (a.pattern != 'fill')

    changed = 0
    for (off, x, y) in pts:
        if mask and data[off] == dominant:
            continue
        if a.pattern == 'stripes':
            idx = cols[((x + y) // 3) % len(cols)]
        elif a.pattern == 'checker':
            idx = cols[((x // 4) + (y // 4)) % len(cols)]
        else:
            idx = cols[0]
        if data[off] != idx:
            changed += 1
        data[off] = idx

    open(a.out, 'wb').write(data)
    print('%s  entry %d (%dx%d, %d pixels, %d changed) indices=%s' %
          (a.out, a.entry, w, h, len(pts), changed, cols))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

```

`port/tools/am04_sheet.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""am04_sheet.py - render every sprite entry in an AM04 file to one PNG grid.

Same layout knowledge as render_am04.py: section E holds pixel indices, F holds
(skip, count) runs, G holds per-row run references, D holds one 0x18-byte entry
per frame with the row index at +0x10 and the width at +0x14.

Usage: python3 am04_sheet.py FILE.bin OUT.png [--cols 13] [--cell 80]
"""
import struct
import sys
import zlib


def png(path, w, h, rows):
    raw = b''.join(b'\x00' + bytes(r) for r in rows)

    def ck(tag, payload):
        return (struct.pack('>I', len(payload)) + tag + payload +
                struct.pack('>I', zlib.crc32(tag + payload) & 0xFFFFFFFF))

    open(path, 'wb').write(
        b'\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n' +
        ck(b'IHDR', struct.pack('>IIBBBBB', w, h, 8, 2, 0, 0, 0)) +
        ck(b'IDAT', zlib.compress(raw, 9)) + ck(b'IEND', b''))


def main():
    src, dst = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    cols = 13
    cell = 80
    if '--cols' in sys.argv:
        cols = int(sys.argv[sys.argv.index('--cols') + 1])
    if '--cell' in sys.argv:
        cell = int(sys.argv[sys.argv.index('--cell') + 1])

    f = open(src, 'rb').read()
    if f[:4] != b'AM04':
        raise SystemExit('not AM04')

    def sec(o):
        return struct.unpack_from('<I', f, o)[0]

    D, E, F, G, P = sec(0x20), sec(0x28), sec(0x30), sec(0x38), sec(0x18)
    pal = []
    for i in range(256):
        c = struct.unpack_from('<I', f, P + i * 4)[0]
        pal.append((c & 255, (c >> 8) & 255, (c >> 16) & 255))

    nent = max(0, (E - D) // 0x18)
    cells = []
    for e in range(nent):
        o = D + e * 0x18
        packed = struct.unpack_from('<I', f, o)[0]
        hh, ww = packed >> 16, packed & 0xFFFF
        row_start = struct.unpack_from('<i', f, o + 0x10)[0]
        ew = struct.unpack_from('<i', f, o + 0x14)[0]
        if not (0 < hh < 300) or not (0 < ew < 300) or row_start < 0:
            cells.append(None)
            continue
        img = [[(0, 0, 0)] * ew for _ in range(hh)]
        for y in range(hh):
            ro = G + (row_start + y) * 8
            if ro + 8 > len(f):
                break
            first, runs = struct.unpack_from('<II', f, ro)
            x = 0
            for r in range(runs):
                do = F + (first + r) * 8
                if do + 8 > len(f):
                    break
                off, sk, ct = struct.unpack_from('<IHH', f, do)
                x += sk
                for i in range(ct):
                    p = E + off + i
                    if p >= len(f) or x >= ew:
                        break
                    img[y][x] = pal[f[p]]
                    x += 1
        cells.append((ww, hh, img))

    rows_n = max(1, (len(cells) + cols - 1) // cols)
    canvas = [[(16, 16, 24)] * (cell * cols) for _ in range(cell * rows_n)]
    for idx, c in enumerate(cells):
        if not c:
            continue
        ww, hh, img = c
        cx = (idx % cols) * cell + 2
        cy = (idx // cols) * cell + 2
        for y in range(min(hh, len(img), cell - 4)):
            for x in range(min(ww, len(img[y]), cell - 4)):
                canvas[cy + y][cx + x] = img[y][x]

    out = []
    for row in canvas:
        line = []
        for px in row:
            line += list(px)
        out.append(line)
    png(dst, cell * cols, cell * rows_n, out)
    print('%s  %dx%d  %d entries (%d drawn)' %
          (dst, cell * cols, cell * rows_n, len(cells), sum(1 for c in cells if c)))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

```

`port/tools/png_scl.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Convert between ordinary image files and the pack's overlay sprite format (.scl).

The game can only draw sprites it knows about, so replacement art is delivered as an
overlay: a named sprite (data file key + entry) plus the pixels to draw in its place.
This tool is the bridge an artist needs - edit a PNG in anything, convert it, drop it
in the pack.

Format (.scl), little endian:

    +0x00  'SCL1'
    +0x04  uint16  width
    +0x06  uint16  height
    +0x08  uint16  scale     1 = draw as authored, 2 = each source pixel becomes 2x2
    +0x0A  uint16  pixels     width*height, RGB565, row major

A pixel of 0x0000 is not drawn (the hook skips it), which is how transparency works.
PNG alpha below --alpha-threshold converts to 0x0000.

Usage:
    png_scl.py to-scl art.png art.scl [--scale N] [--alpha-threshold N]
    png_scl.py to-png art.scl art.png
    png_scl.py info art.scl

Requires ffmpeg on PATH (used for PNG encode/decode so there is no Pillow dependency).
"""

import argparse
import os
import struct
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile

MAGIC = b"SCL1"


def _run(args):
    proc = subprocess.run(args, capture_output=True)
    if proc.returncode != 0:
        raise RuntimeError((proc.stderr or b"").decode("utf-8", "replace").strip()[-300:]
                           or "ffmpeg failed")
    return proc.stdout


def _probe(path):
    out = _run(["ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-select_streams", "v:0",
                "-show_entries", "stream=width,height", "-of", "csv=p=0", path])
    w, h = out.decode().strip().split(",")[:2]
    return int(w), int(h)


def to_scl(src, dst, scale=1, alpha_threshold=128):
    """Image file -> .scl. Any format ffmpeg can read."""
    w, h = _probe(src)
    raw = _run(["ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-i", src, "-f", "rawvideo",
                "-pix_fmt", "rgba", "-"])
    expected = w * h * 4
    if len(raw) < expected:
        raise RuntimeError("short read from %s (%d of %d bytes)" % (src, len(raw), expected))

    body = bytearray(MAGIC)
    body += struct.pack("<HHH", w, h, scale)

    transparent = 0
    for i in range(0, expected, 4):
        r, g, b, a = raw[i], raw[i + 1], raw[i + 2], raw[i + 3]
        if a < alpha_threshold:
            body += b"\x00\x00"
            transparent += 1
            continue
        body += struct.pack("<H", ((r >> 3) << 11) | ((g >> 2) << 5) | (b >> 3))

    with open(dst, "wb") as fh:
        fh.write(bytes(body))

    return {"width": w, "height": h, "scale": scale,
            "bytes": len(body), "transparent": transparent}


def read_scl(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
        data = fh.read()
    if len(data) < 10 or data[:4] != MAGIC:
        raise RuntimeError("%s is not an .scl file" % path)
    w, h, scale = struct.unpack_from("<HHH", data, 4)
    pixels = data[10:]
    if len(pixels) < w * h * 2:
        raise RuntimeError("%s is truncated: %d of %d pixel bytes"
                           % (path, len(pixels), w * h * 2))
    return w, h, scale, pixels


def to_png(src, dst):
    """ .scl -> image file, so art can be inspected or edited further."""
    w, h, scale, pixels = read_scl(src)

    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
        raw_path = os.path.join(td, "art.rgba")
        with open(raw_path, "wb") as fh:
            for i in range(w * h):
                value = struct.unpack_from("<H", pixels, i * 2)[0]
                if value == 0:
                    fh.write(b"\x00\x00\x00\x00")
                    continue
                r = ((value >> 11) & 0x1F) * 255 // 31
                g = ((value >> 5) & 0x3F) * 255 // 63
                b = (value & 0x1F) * 255 // 31
                fh.write(bytes((r, g, b, 255)))
        _run(["ffmpeg", "-v", "error", "-y", "-f", "rawvideo", "-pix_fmt", "rgba",
              "-s", "%dx%d" % (w, h), "-i", raw_path, "-pix_fmt", "rgb24", dst])

    return {"width": w, "height": h, "scale": scale}


def main(argv):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="pack overlay sprite converter")
    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="cmd", required=True)

    p1 = sub.add_parser("to-scl", help="PNG (or any image) -> .scl")
    p1.add_argument("src")
    p1.add_argument("dst")
    p1.add_argument("--scale", type=int, default=1,
                    help="1 = native size (layout safe), 2+ enlarges on screen")
    p1.add_argument("--alpha-threshold", type=int, default=128,
                    help="alpha below this becomes transparent")

    p2 = sub.add_parser("to-png", help=".scl -> PNG")
    p2.add_argument("src")
    p2.add_argument("dst")

    p3 = sub.add_parser("info", help="describe an .scl")
    p3.add_argument("src")

    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    try:
        if args.cmd == "to-scl":
            info = to_scl(args.src, args.dst, args.scale, args.alpha_threshold)
            print("%s -> %s" % (args.src, args.dst))
            print("  %(width)dx%(height)d, scale %(scale)d, %(bytes)d bytes, "
                  "%(transparent)d transparent pixels" % info)
            if args.scale != 1:
                print("  note: scale %d draws %dx larger than the sprite cell and will "
                      "overflow its layout" % (args.scale, args.scale))
        elif args.cmd == "to-png":
            info = to_png(args.src, args.dst)
            print("%s -> %s  (%dx%d, scale %d)"
                  % (args.src, args.dst, info["width"], info["height"], info["scale"]))
        else:
            w, h, scale, pixels = read_scl(args.src)
            opaque = sum(1 for i in range(w * h)
                         if struct.unpack_from("<H", pixels, i * 2)[0] != 0)
            print("%s: %dx%d, scale %d, %d/%d opaque pixels"
                  % (args.src, w, h, scale, opaque, w * h))
    except Exception as exc:
        print("error: %s" % exc, file=sys.stderr)
        return 1

    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

```

`port/tools/render_am04.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""render_am04.py - render a sprite from an AM04 file, outside the game.

Transcribed from the blitter FUN_00404b20 (original 1.04). Field mapping was
corrected by reading FUN_00409050, which builds the five pointers passed to it:

    param_1[0] = &image[0x18]   -> *param_1 = palette records (0x230 stride)
    param_1[1] = image[0x20]    -> section D: 0x18-byte entries
    param_1[2] = image[0x38]    -> section G: row table, 8 bytes/row
    param_1[3] = image[0x30]    -> section F: run descriptors, 8 bytes/run
    param_1[4] = image[0x28]    -> section E: pixel indices

Row table entry  (8 bytes): +0 uint32 first descriptor, +4 uint32 run count
Descriptor entry (8 bytes): +0 uint32 offset into E, +4 uint16 skip, +6 uint16 count

Usage:
    python3 render_am04.py FILE.bin --entry N --height H --out OUT.png
"""
import argparse
import struct
import zlib


def read_sections(data):
    names = {'A': 0x08, 'B': 0x10, 'pal': 0x18, 'D': 0x20, 'E': 0x28, 'F': 0x30, 'G': 0x38}
    return {k: struct.unpack_from('<I', data, v)[0] for k, v in names.items()}


def write_png(path, w, h, rows):
    raw = b''.join(b'\x00' + bytes(r) for r in rows)
    def chunk(tag, payload):
        c = struct.pack('>I', len(payload)) + tag + payload
        return c + struct.pack('>I', zlib.crc32(tag + payload) & 0xFFFFFFFF)
    png = b'\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n'
    png += chunk(b'IHDR', struct.pack('>IIBBBBB', w, h, 8, 2, 0, 0, 0))
    png += chunk(b'IDAT', zlib.compress(raw, 9))
    png += chunk(b'IEND', b'')
    open(path, 'wb').write(png)


def palette_rgb(data, sec, index):
    rec = sec['pal'] + index * 0x400
    out = []
    for i in range(256):
        c = struct.unpack_from('<I', data, rec + i * 4)[0]
        out.append((c & 0xFF, (c >> 8) & 0xFF, (c >> 16) & 0xFF))
    return out


def render(data, entry, height, pal_index=0, width=None):
    sec = read_sections(data)
    pal = palette_rgb(data, sec, pal_index)
    D, E, F, G = sec['D'], sec['E'], sec['F'], sec['G']

    row_start = struct.unpack_from('<i', data, D + entry * 0x18 + 0x10)[0]
    entry_w = struct.unpack_from('<i', data, D + entry * 0x18 + 0x14)[0]

    rows = []
    maxw = 0
    for y in range(height):
        row_off = G + (row_start + y) * 8
        if row_off + 8 > len(data):
            break
        first, runs = struct.unpack_from('<II', data, row_off)
        if runs == 0 or runs > 4096:
            break

        canvas = {}
        x = 0
        ok = True

        for r in range(runs):
            d_off = F + (first + r) * 8
            if d_off + 8 > len(data):
                ok = False
                break
            offset, skip, count = struct.unpack_from('<IHH', data, d_off)
            x += skip
            for i in range(count):
                p = E + offset + i
                if p >= len(data):
                    ok = False
                    break
                canvas[x] = pal[data[p]]
                x += 1
            if not ok:
                break

        if not canvas:
            rows.append(None)
            continue

        w = max(max(canvas) + 1, entry_w if 0 < entry_w < 4096 else 0)
        if w > maxw:
            maxw = w
        line = [0] * (w * 3)
        for px, rgb in canvas.items():
            line[px * 3:px * 3 + 3] = list(rgb)
        rows.append(line)

    if width:
        maxw = max(maxw, width)
    if maxw == 0:
        raise SystemExit('no rows rendered - entry/height likely wrong')
    out = []
    for r in rows:
        if r is None:
            out.append([0] * (maxw * 3))
        else:
            pad = maxw * 3 - len(r)
            out.append(r + [0] * pad)
    return maxw, len(out), out


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument('file')
    ap.add_argument('--entry', type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument('--height', type=int, default=64)
    ap.add_argument('--palette', type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument('--width', type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument('--out', default='sprite.png')
    a = ap.parse_args()

    data = open(a.file, 'rb').read()
    w, h, rows = render(data, a.entry, a.height, a.palette, a.width or None)
    write_png(a.out, w, h, rows)
    print('%s  %dx%d  entry=%d' % (a.out, w, h, a.entry))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

```
